### RL-51: Medium (process; Thurgood; not tag-relevant): the fallback question on the records' home was not put to Peter at the second release

**Update (2026-11-12, Stacy): ruled.** Peter, 2026-11-12T13:25:17Z: "(b), move now in one ballot" (`.kiro/specs/129-consumer-generation-completeness/evidence/2026-11-12-peter-ruling-records-home-b.md`). The release records and the § 5.3 delegate file move to `.kiro/docs/releases/<v>/` now, without waiting for Spec 131's owners file. The vehicle is one ballot drafted by Thurgood and ratified by Peter, record-first. It carries the five seats' standing write scope over `.kiro/docs/releases/**`, the RELEASE-FLOW step 6 switch, and the capture record's close. The ruling directs the vehicle and ratifies no text yet written. Records already written, this one included, stay where they are unless the new ballot says otherwise. My consult position was (c), the interim home plus a one-site ballot, and Peter ruled (b). The finding text below is kept as first written.

- RELEASE-FLOW step 6, "Where a release's records live", fallback bullet: "at the walk of the second release whose walk record's first `main at:` SHA descends from that ballot's application merge … the capture record's re-rule PR at that release carries one `**Open for Peter**:` line … standing write scope for the five seats now (that ballot's option (b)), or the interim home for this release."
- 16.3.0 is that second release (#559 `a4e7fa12c` is an ancestor of `c3fd1893a`; 16.2.0's `2c00cea49` was the first).
- No re-rule PR for `2026-10-29-release-records-home-switch.md` exists in the delta. The file has no commit in `v16.2.0..S`. #851's body has no `**Open for Peter**:` line. The walk rows the record `carried` without naming a hold.
- **Consequences, as the law states them**: the release goes ahead at the interim home, and the capture record lapses at S (table above).
- **Phase 1 reports the open question**: I put it to Peter as `**Open for Peter**:` line 2 in this PR. The law's vehicle for it is the capture record's re-rule PR, and that is Thurgood's.
- **Routed**: to Thurgood (the owner): the re-rule PR, with the line, at the next walk.

- The finding text below is kept as first written.

- **My reading, offered**: the deletions are clean, and the guard is green at S. The header is the adoption baseline, though, and the RELEASE pass compares P against it. Rewriting it to the post-fix count makes the baseline say 72 where 75 were adopted.
- **Routed**: to Lina (the author) and Thurgood (the instrument), and to Peter (`**Open for Peter**:` line 4, with RL-60).

## Method

- **Where I read**: in my own worktree at S (`scratchpad/stacy-rel163`, detached at `6f507c039`), from git (`git show`, `git log --first-parent`, `git diff-tree`, `git ls-tree`).
  - `node_modules` is symlinked from the main checkout, and `npm run build` ran in the worktree (exit 0) for the guard and the pointer check.
  - PR bodies and `refs/pull/<n>/head` came from GitHub, read with credentials from the main checkout's `.env` in the same call. The token was never printed.
  - Nothing tracked changed except this PR's three files (`git status --porcelain`).
  - Scratch outputs are in `scratchpad/stacy163/` (bodies, reads, greps, owed set, walk outputs, `ekp.txt`).
- **Handed over and accepted as relayed**: the 5.1 log and its timing files (I read the log myself and `shasum`'d the tarball), and Peter's chat words as the ruling records quote them.
- **What I did not re-verify, by name**:
  - `validate` on a consumer at S;
  - I-1 and I-2 on native (toolchain unavailable);
  - the F2 byte compare;
  - the notes' item 3 against the ruling, line by line;
  - the trio tallies (records' own lines);
  - GitHub Packages;
  - the straggler sections of #816 and #823;
  - the census lines of the window's ballots;
  - fired non-due tokens per seat;
  - per-seat assent tallies for non-Stacy signers;
  - J.
- **No unverifiable claim was rolled into a verdict.** Each "fixed", "true" and "lapses" above cites the command or the line read at S.

6. Phase 2, at the step-6 PR's merge.

## Standards implications

`Standards implications: list`. These are for Thurgood's full read under the composed loop. Any repair is co-drafted and authored by him.
- **S-1**: a walk "carried" row is not a re-rule, and #851's plan that owners re-rule "before S" had no instrument (RL-52 (a)). Class option: the walk's own PR runs the lapse projection (the B2 table, by script) and pastes it. 16.2.0's walk had one, and 16.3.0's does not.
- **S-2**: step 5's pastes can be dropped by a release PR that lists them as "owed before S" (RL-50). Class option: the release PR's required check, or the pre-S checklist, runs `owed-set.sh` and the arming grep, and fails or warns on a missing paste.
- **S-3**: a floor promise made in prose ("they go to Peter's floor-class batch") reached no `**Open for Peter**:` line (RL-49). Class option: the walk's conditional floor rows are written as rows with a state, `conditional — <PR>`, which phase 1 resolves.
- **S-4**: a `release:next:phase-1` record filed in the window between the walk's grep and the walk's merge (here 28 s) is neither rowed nor excepted (link7-f2b). Rule 11's post-walk exception keys on the walk's merge, while the walk's paste keys on its `main at:`.
- **S-5**: RS-41's "a sentence its record has since made false" does not say whether a dated plan line in an earlier release's entry can be made false.
- **S-6**: the non-Stacy signing flags at RELEASE (RL-60) have no named reader in § 5.1. § 5.3 names Peter for Stacy-signed flags only.

## Forced negative (this pass)

- **One floor-class row was unwaived at S** (RL-49). Peter waived it for 16.3.0 at 2026-11-10T01:59:34Z, and the fix is owed by the next release's S. Every other floor surface the walk read is fixed at S.
- **No ❌ in any criterion row.** No parent criteria were re-read here (they were read at the CLOSEOUT passes).
- **Unverifiable claims are named, not counted green**: see § "Method".
- **Fifteen records lapse on a reading whose deadline has passed** (RL-52). The lapses are reported, not acted on, except on my own record.

## Surviving counter-argument to my own verdict

My verdict is that RL-49 holds the tag. It rests on reading two worked-example lines as a P0. The author reads them as not P0. On the plain meaning of P0 ("a statement false … where a reader of the package meets it"), a reasonable reader could side with him: "`Icon.test.ts` - Tests functional API" is a teaching example in a standards doc, and the agent who meets it will not be misled about anything the package does.
- **What I folded in**: I do not rest the hold on my P0 reading alone. It rests on the walk's and the owner's own written conditional, which put these lines in Peter's batch whatever the reading, and on 3a's rule that only Peter waives. I also separated the 32 fence calls, which I read as illustrations, from the two named lines.
- **What survives**: if Peter reads the conditional as the author's promise and not as law, the hold is one I raised on a narrow reading, and it delays a tag over a stale file name. The cost of being wrong in that direction is one waiver line. The cost of the other direction is a charter shipped with names that do not exist, which the walk said it would not do. The pick is Peter's.
